> AGENT

We don’t strictly need them. They’re not methods, just local helper functions used to avoid duplicating shared Codex CLI argument policy between launch and restore.

Right now both [GetLaunchCommand](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex.go:57) and [GetRestoreCommand](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex.go:93) need the same pieces:

- disable Codex update checks
- map `agent.PermissionMode` to Codex approval flags
- append model flags, including `model_reasoning_effort=high` for `o3` / `o4` models

We could inline those appends directly into both command builders and it would work. The downside is duplicated command-shape logic, especially [appendModelFlags](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex.go:257), which has non-obvious policy attached.

One Go-specific detail: because `append` returns a new slice header, a helper cannot just take `cmd []string` and append locally unless it returns the new slice. That is why these helpers currently take `*[]string`. A more idiomatic shape would be:

```go
cmd = appendModelFlags(cmd, model)

func appendModelFlags(cmd []string, model string) []string {
    if model == "" {
        return cmd
    }
    cmd = append(cmd, "--model", model)
    if oSeriesModelPattern.MatchString(model) {
        cmd = append(cmd, "-c", "model_reasoning_effort=high")
    }
    return cmd
}
```

So: direct inline appends are valid; helpers exist for deduplication. If keeping helpers, returning `[]string` would probably read cleaner than mutating `*[]string`.

   223	func (p *Plugin) codexBinary(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
   224		p.binaryMu.Lock()
   225		defer p.binaryMu.Unlock()
   226	
   227		if p.resolvedBinary != "" {

   229		}
   230	
   231		binary, err := ResolveCodexBinary(ctx)
   232		if err != nil {

   235		p.resolvedBinary = binary
   236		return binary, nil
   237	}
   238	
   239	func appendNoUpdateCheckFlag(cmd *[]string) {
   240		*cmd = append(*cmd, "-c", "check_for_update_on_startup=false")
   241	}
   242	
   243	func appendApprovalFlags(cmd *[]string, permissions agent.PermissionMode) {
   244		switch normalizePermissionMode(permissions) {
   245		case agent.PermissionModePermissionless:
   246			*cmd = append(*cmd, "--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox")
   247		case agent.PermissionModeAutoEdit:
   248			*cmd = append(*cmd, "--ask-for-approval", "never")
   249		case agent.PermissionModeSuggest:
   250			*cmd = append(*cmd, "--ask-for-approval", "untrusted")
   251		}
   252	}
   253	
   254	func appendModelFlags(cmd *[]string, model string) {
   255		if model == "" {
   256			return
   257		}
   258	
   259		*cmd = append(*cmd, "--model", model)
   260		modelPrefix := strings.ToLower(model)
   261		if strings.HasPrefix(modelPrefix, "o3") || strings.HasPrefix(modelPrefix, "o4") {
   262			*cmd = append(*cmd, "-c", "model_reasoning_effort=high")
   263		}
   264	}
   265	
   266	func normalizePermissionMode(mode agent.PermissionMode) agent.PermissionMode {
   267		switch mode {
   268		case "skip":
   269			return agent.PermissionModePermissionless
   270		case agent.PermissionModePermissionless, agent.PermissionModeDefault, agent.PermissionModeAutoEdit, agent.PermissionModeSuggest:
   271			return mode
   272		default:
   273			return ""
   274		}
   275	}

   287		if threadID != "" {

   289				return path, true, nil
   290			}

97:- The durable Better-AO agent contract now lives in `internal/plugin/agent/agent.go`, and built-in agent plugins were moved under `internal/plugin/agent/claude` and `internal/plugin/agent/codex`; `internal/app/app.go` is the live built-in registration call site that had to follow the move [Task 1]
100:- The live hook file in this checkout is `.codex/hooks.json`, with `SessionStart`, `Stop`, and `UserPromptSubmit` calling `entire hooks codex ...`; this is the concrete pre-existing hook behavior to preserve when designing Better-AO’s Codex integration [Task 2] [chronicle memory]
101:- The current app already has one canonical workspace model: backend serves `/api/workspace`, frontend polls it via TanStack Query every 5s, and `orchestrator-home-page.tsx` projects that data into Kanban/sidebar. Realtime activity should patch that same model via a backend-owned in-memory broker/stream rather than inventing a second canonical UI tree [Task 2]
103:- `var _ SomeInterface = (*Type)(nil)` is compile-time only. In this repo, the `plugin.Plugin` assertion on the Codex plugin is mostly redundant because `codex.New()` is already registered through `[]plugin.Plugin` in `internal/app/app.go`, while the `agent.Agent` assertion still acts as the useful early tripwire [Task 3]
104:- `codexThreadId` and `codexModel` are the stable metadata keys that carry native Codex session identity across launch, restore, and session-info flows; `maxSessionScanDepth` and `prefixLineLimit` are safety/performance bounds for transcript discovery under `~/.codex/sessions`; `oSeriesModelPattern` is the plugin-specific assumption that `o3`/`o4` models need `model_reasoning_effort=high` [Task 4]
105:- Before tab removal, the home UI still kept a `view: 'kanban' | 'terminal'` model in `session-view-tabs.tsx`, persisted it in `workspace-preferences.ts`, and switched branches in `session-workspace-panel.tsx`; the grounded direction was Kanban as home/overview plus session-driven terminal routing [Task 5]
106:- `ResolveCodexBinary(ctx)` currently mixes `exec.LookPath("codex")` with manual fallback paths and caches the winning result in `p.resolvedBinary` behind `binaryMu`; `GetLaunchCommand` and `GetRestoreCommand` both reuse that cached resolution [Task 6]
107:- `exec.LookPath("codex")` is the Go-standard baseline for command lookup, but there is no single portable API for a canonical “system PATH”; if the goal is terminal-like PATH, the repo must explicitly source it from the chosen shell/service environment rather than assume the server process already has it [Task 6]
109:- Codex session resume does not invent thread IDs; it first checks session metadata for `codexThreadId` and matches a `-<threadId>.jsonl` filename suffix, otherwise scans `~/.codex/sessions` for `.jsonl` files whose `session_meta.cwd` matches the workspace path through `comparablePath(...)` [Task 7]
110:- `streamCodexSessionData(...)` treats `session_meta` as the primary source for `payload.id` / `payload.threadId`, lets `turn_context` contribute `model`, and writes the recovered values back into metadata as `codexThreadId` / `codexModel` [Task 7]
117:- Symptom: activity-plan drafts drift into persistence/history ideas. Cause: hook events were treated like audit logs instead of current-state publishers. Fix: keep the source of truth in memory and model hooks as publishers of current state only [Task 2]
119:- Symptom: tab-removal advice stays at product-opinion level and misses the real migration work. Cause: the current files that still own `kanban | terminal` state were skipped. Fix: trace `orchestrator-home-page.tsx`, `session-view-tabs.tsx`, and `session-workspace-panel.tsx` first, then recommend the route model from that starting point [Task 5]
122:- Symptom: a repo-local helper keeps accumulating tiny generic utilities in feature files. Cause: the first relocation stopped at `internal/plugin/agent/codex/utils.go` instead of checking whether the helper belonged at shared internal scope. Fix: look for or create a shared `internal/utils` seam before leaving generic helpers next to the plugin code [Task 7]

> AGENT

The current blast radius is small but it crosses the public agent config structs and Codex tests. I’m going to remove `Model` from launch/restore config, stop persisting model as command input, and keep transcript model parsing only as informational session metadata/summary unless tests show that should go too.

   527	func comparablePath(path string) string {
   528		if path == "" {
   529			return ""
   530		}
   531	
   532		slash := strings.ReplaceAll(path, "\\", "/")
   533		if len(slash) >= 2 && slash[1] == ':' {
   534			return strings.ToLower(slash[:1]) + slash[1:]
   535		}
   536	
   537		return slash
   538	}

Agreed; this was the wrong ownership boundary.

I removed the model-selection path from the agent plugin contract and Codex command builder:

- [launch.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/launch.go:4): removed `LaunchConfig.Model`
- [restore.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/restore.go:4): removed `RestoreConfig.Model`
- [codex.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex.go:53): launch/restore no longer emit `--model` or `model_reasoning_effort`
- [codex.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex.go:115): session metadata now tracks only the Codex thread id, not the model
- [codex_test.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex_test.go:14): updated expectations so model flags are absent

Verified with `go test ./...`.

          Disable a feature (repeatable). Equivalent to `-c features.<name>=false`

      --remote <ADDR>
          Connect the TUI to a remote app server websocket endpoint.
          
          Accepted forms: `ws://host:port` or `wss://host:port`.

      --remote-auth-token-env <ENV_VAR>
          Name of the environment variable containing the bearer token to send to a remote app
          server websocket

  -i, --image <FILE>...
          Optional image(s) to attach to the initial prompt

  -m, --model <MODEL>
          Model the agent should use

      --oss
          Use open-source provider

      --local-provider <OSS_PROVIDER>
          Specify which local provider to use (lmstudio or ollama). If not specified with --oss,
          will use config default or show selection

  -p, --profile <CONFIG_PROFILE>
          Configuration profile from config.toml to specify default options

  -s, --sandbox <SANDBOX_MODE>
          Select the sandbox policy to use when executing model-generated shell commands
          
          [possible values: read-only, workspace-write, danger-full-access]

      --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox
          Skip all confirmation prompts and execute commands without sandboxing. EXTREMELY
          DANGEROUS. Intended solely for running in environments that are externally sandboxed

  -C, --cd <DIR>
          Tell the agent to use the specified directory as its working root

      --add-dir <DIR>
          Additional directories that should be writable alongside the primary workspace

  -a, --ask-for-approval <APPROVAL_POLICY>
          Configure when the model requires human approval before executing a command

          Possible values:
          - untrusted:  Only run "trusted" commands (e.g. ls, cat, sed) without asking for user
            approval. Will escalate to the user if the model proposes a command that is not in the
            "trusted" set
          - on-failure: DEPRECATED: Run all commands without asking for user approval. Only asks for
            approval if a command fails to execute, in which case it will escalate to the user to
            ask for un-sandboxed execution. Prefer `on-request` for interactive runs or `never` for
            non-interactive runs
          - on-request: The model decides when to ask the user for approval
          - never:      Never ask for user approval Execution failures are immediately returned to
            the model

      --search
          Enable live web search. When enabled, the native Responses `web_search` tool is available
          to the model (no per‑call approval)

      --no-alt-screen
          Disable alternate screen mode
          
          Runs the TUI in inline mode, preserving terminal scrollback history. This is useful in
          terminal multiplexers like Zellij that follow the xterm spec strictly and disable
          scrollback in alternate screen buffers.

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

> AGENT

`appendApprovalFlags` is doing one thing: translating Better-AO’s generic `agent.PermissionMode` into Codex-specific CLI args in both launch and restore paths.

The mapping is currently:

- `permissionless` -> `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`
- `auto-edit` -> `--ask-for-approval never`
- `suggest` -> `--ask-for-approval untrusted`
- `default` / unknown -> no flags
- legacy `"skip"` -> `permissionless`

I checked `codex --help` and `codex resume --help`; those flags are currently valid for both launch and resume. The deeper problem is not CLI validity. It is ownership and semantics.

The weak points:

1. `permissionless` is not just “no approval prompts”; it also disables sandboxing. That is a much stronger security change than the generic enum name communicates.

2. `auto-edit` only sets approval behavior. It does not define sandbox mode, so its real behavior still depends on the user’s Codex config.

3. `suggest` -> `untrusted` is not a clean semantic match. Codex still runs trusted commands without asking; it is not “suggest only” in a product sense.

4. `normalizePermissionMode` silently treats invalid values as no flags. That makes a typo behave like default config instead of failing clearly.

5. The `"skip"` alias is compatibility debt inside the Codex plugin. I do not see a current live caller outside the test, so it probably should not live here.

My recommendation: if we are applying the same boundary as model selection, delete `Permissions` from `LaunchConfig` / `RestoreConfig`, remove `appendApprovalFlags` and `normalizePermissionMode`, and let the user’s Codex config/profile own approval and sandbox policy. If Better-AO must own this as a cross-agent safety policy later, it should be explicit and error-returning, not a silent helper in the Codex plugin.

> DEVELOPER

The orchestrator will handle CI monitoring and review routing.\n- If you're told to take over or continue work on an existing PR, run `ao session claim-pr <pr-number-or-url>` from inside this session before making changes.\n- If CI fails, the orchestrator will send you the failures — fix them and push again.\n- If reviewers request changes, the orchestrator will forward their comments — address each one, push fixes, and reply to the comments.\n\n## Git Workflow\n- Always create a feature branch from the default branch (never commit directly to it).\n- Use conventional commit messages (feat:, fix:, chore:, etc.).\n- Push your branch and create a PR when the implementation is ready.\n- Keep PRs focused — one issue per PR.\n\n## PR Best Practices\n- Write a clear PR title and description explaining what changed and why.\n- Link the issue in the PR description so it auto-closes when merged.\n- If the repo has CI checks, make sure they pass before requesting review.\n- Respond to every review comment, even if just to acknowledge it.\n\n## Project Context\n- Project: atlanta\n- Repository: yyovil/agent-orchestrator\n- Default branch: main\n- Tracker: github\n\n## Project Rules\nAlways run tests before pushing.\nUse conventional commits (feat:, fix:, chore:, docs:, refactor:, test:).\nLink issue numbers in commit messages.\nWrite clear commit messages that explain WHY, not just WHAT.\n\nUse TypeScript strict mode.\nUse ESM modules with .js extensions in imports (e.g., import { foo } from \"./bar.js\").\nUse node: prefix for built-in modules (e.g., import { readFile } from \"node:fs\").\nPrefer const over let, never use var.\nUse type imports for type-only imports: import type { Foo } from \"./bar.js\".\nNo any types - use unknown with type guards instead.\n\nThis is a pnpm monorepo with workspaces.\nRun commands from root with pnpm -r (recursive) or from specific package directories.\nBefore pushing: pnpm build && pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test.\nAlways build packages before running dependent packages.\n\nBefore pushing, run these commands:\n- pnpm build (build all packages)\n- pnpm typecheck (type check all packages)\n- pnpm lint (or pnpm lint:fix to auto-fix)\n\n## Additional Instructions\nDo nothing. Stay idle and wait for further instructions. Do not make any code changes, run tests, or open PRs unless explicitly told later."}

read-onlyworkspace-writedanger-full-accessraw_overridesuntrustedOnly run "trusted" commands (e.g. ls, cat, sed) without asking for user approval. Will escalate to the user if the model proposes a command that is not in the "trusted" seton-failureDEPRECATED: Run all commands without asking for user approval. Only asks for approval if a command fails to execute, in which case it will escalate to the user to ask for un-sandboxed execution. Prefer `on-request` for interactive runs or `never` for non-interactive runson-requestThe model decides when to ask the user for approvalneverNever ask for user approval Execution failures are immediately returned to the model

The stale enum values are gone from the public Go contract. I kept legacy string aliases inside `normalizePermissionMode` only, so older `"skip"`, `"yolo"`, or `"permissionless"` inputs normalize to `full-access` instead of leaking into the contract.

> AGENT

Updated the permission model to the three Codex modes:

- `default`
- `auto-review`
- `full-access`

Changes:
- [permissions.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/permissions.go:7) now exposes only `PermissionModeDefault`, `PermissionModeAutoReview`, and `PermissionModeFullAccess`.
- [codex.go](/Users/tanishqpalandurkar/Projects/better-ao/internal/plugin/agent/codex/codex.go:228) maps:
  - `default` -> `--sandbox workspace-write --ask-for-approval on-request`
  - `auto-review` -> same as default plus `-c approvals_reviewer="auto_review"`
  - `full-access` -> `--sandbox danger-full-access --ask-for-approval never`
- `normalizePermissionMode` still accepts legacy `"skip"`, `"yolo"`, `"permissionless"`, and `"full"` as aliases for `full-access`.
- Empty permissions still mean “do not override Codex config.”

Verified with `go test ./...`.

I checked this against the installed `codex --help` and the official Codex docs for CLI flags and auto-review behavior: [CLI reference](https://developers.openai.com/codex/cli/reference), [Auto-review](https://developers.openai.com/codex/concepts/sandboxing/auto-review).
